âš ï¸ Gotchas

Problema: npm install en cada worktree â†’ triplicado el espacio

SoluciÃ³n: Usa npm ci (respeta lock file) o considera monorepo tools (pnpm, yarn workspaces)

Problema: Conflicto con node_modules compartido

SoluciÃ³n: Ignora node_modules en git, instala localmente por worktree

Estrategias Avanzadas: Rebase & Squash

Para: Mantener histories limpias en repositorios grandes

Interactive Rebase: Reorganiza Commits Antes de Merge

# SupÃ³n que tienes 5 commits con mensajes malos:
git log --oneline main..HEAD
# a1b2c3d fix: typo
# b2c3d4e feat: TOTP setup
# c3d4e5f fix: linting error
# d4e5f6g feat: TOTP recovery codes
# e5f6g7h fix: test warnings

# Abre editor interactivo:
git rebase -i main
# En el editor aparece:
# pick a1b2c3d fix: typo
# pick b2c3d4e feat: TOTP setup
# pick c3d4e5f fix: linting error
# pick d4e5f6g feat: TOTP recovery codes
# pick e5f6g7h fix: test warnings

# Reorganiza y fusiona:
# pick b2c3d4e feat: TOTP setup
# squash c3d4e5f fix: linting error
# squash a1b2c3d fix: typo
# pick d4e5f6g feat: TOTP recovery codes
# squash e5f6g7h fix: test warnings

# Resultado:
# - 1 commit: TOTP setup + fixes
# - 1 commit: TOTP recovery codes + fixes

# Git abre editor para coalescar mensajes. Limpia:
# feat: TOTP 2FA implementation
# - Setup flow with QR code
# - Recovery codes for account recovery
# - Full test coverage (12 cases)

git rebase --continue  # Si hay conflicts, git add . && git rebase --continue

Squash Merge: 1 Commit por Feature

# En vez de:
git merge feature/otp-auth
# Que genera: Merge pull request #42 (mantiene todos los commits)

# Usa squash:
git merge --squash feature/otp-auth
git commit -m "feat: TOTP 2FA implementation"

# Resultado en main:
# Solo 1 commit limpio, sin histÃ³rico de "fix: typo", "fix: lint", etc.

# En GitHub/GitLab:
# PR settings â†’ "Squash and merge" (opciÃ³n por defecto para muchos)

# Ventaja: main history limpio
# Desventaja: Pierdes granularidad de commits individuales

Cherry-Pick: Copia Commits Selectivos

# Hotfix en main, necesita estar en develop tambiÃ©n:
git log --oneline main | head -3
# abc1234 fix: TOTP secret validation
# def5678 chore: v2.5.1
# ghi9012 Merge hotfix/totp-secret...

# Copia ese commit a develop:
git checkout develop
git cherry-pick abc1234

# Si hay conflicts:
git add .
git cherry-pick --continue

# Ãštil para:
# 1. Hotfixes â†’ main + develop
# 2. Backport de features a versiones antiguas
# 3. Compartir commits entre release branches

Troubleshooting: Merge Conflicts & Recovery

Conflictos de Merge: Caso Real

# Intenta mergear feature/auth a develop:
git checkout develop && git pull origin develop
git merge feature/auth
# CONFLICT (content): Merge conflict in src/auth/middleware.ts
# CONFLICT (content): Merge conflict in package.json
# Automatic merge failed; fix conflicts and then commit the result.

# 1. Ver el estado:
git status
# both modified: src/auth/middleware.ts
# both modified: package.json

# 2. Abre los archivos con conflictos:
cat src/auth/middleware.ts
# <<<<<<< HEAD (develop)
# export const AUTH_TIMEOUT = 3600;
# =======
# export const AUTH_TIMEOUT = 7200;
# >>>>>>> feature/auth
#
# Los dos cambios son vÃ¡lidos. Elige:

# 3. Resuelve manualmente:
# OpciÃ³n A: Usar VSCode (UI para resolver)
code src/auth/middleware.ts
# Click "Accept Current Change" o "Accept Incoming Change"

# OpciÃ³n B: Terminal
# MantÃ©n ambos cambios
export const AUTH_TIMEOUT = 7200;  // Nueva en feature
// Comentario de develop
# O elige uno:
export const AUTH_TIMEOUT = 7200;  // MantÃ©n feature

# 4. Mark como resolved:
git add src/auth/middleware.ts
git add package.json

# 5. Completa merge:
git commit -m "Merge feature/auth into develop
- Resuelve timeout conflict: 3600 â†’ 7200 (feature)
- Actualiza dependencies segÃºn feature/auth"

# 6. Push:
git push origin develop

Abortar Merge en Progreso

# Si el merge se pone feo:
git merge --abort

# Si ya hiciste commit (oops):
git reset --soft HEAD~1  # Deshace commit, mantiene cambios
git reset --hard HEAD~1  # Deshace todo (peligroso, perderÃ¡s cambios)

Stash: Pausa Trabajo Temporal

# EstÃ¡s en feature/auth con cambios sin commitear
# Necesitas cambiar a main para hotfix

# Guardar todo:
git stash save "WIP: TOTP flow incomplete"

# Cambiar de rama sin problemas:
git checkout main

# ... hace hotfix ...

# Vuelve a feature/auth:
git checkout feature/auth

# Recupera tu stash:
git stash pop

# O ver el stash:
git stash list
git stash show stash@{0}  # Ver quÃ© hay
git stash apply stash@{0}  # Aplicar sin eliminar

CoordinaciÃ³n de Equipo: SincronizaciÃ³n

# Al iniciar el dÃ­a:

# Si tu branch estÃ¡ detrÃ¡s:

# Equivalente: git fetch origin && git merge origin/feature/otp-auth

# Si develop avanzÃ³ y necesitas actualizar tu feature:

# Resuelve conflicts si hay

Crear Alias para Flujos Comunes

[alias]
  # Trunk-based
  sync = "!git fetch origin && git rebase origin/main"
  new = "!sh -c 'git checkout -b $1 && git push -u origin' -"
  done = "!git checkout main && git pull && git branch -d"

  # Gitflow

  # Info

  authors = "!git shortlog -sn"

# Uso:

ProducciÃ³n / Hooks en ProducciÃ³n

ðŸŽ£ Hooks para Seguridad & AutomaciÃ³n

8 hooks reales que automatizan seguridad, tests, monitoreo y manejo de errores en ciclo completo de ejecuciÃ³n.

ðŸ“‹ VisiÃ³n General: 8 Hooks Disponibles

Cada hook se ejecuta en un momento especÃ­fico del ciclo de vida de Claude Code. Selecciona el que necesites:

ðŸ”’
Security Audit
PreToolUse
Valida comandos peligrosos antes de ejecutar
âœ…
Auto-Format + Tests

Formatea cÃ³digo y corre tests automÃ¡ticamente
ðŸ’‰

ðŸ’°

ðŸš€

ðŸ’¾

ðŸ›¡ï¸

â±ï¸

ðŸ”„ Hook Lifecycle: CuÃ¡ndo se ejecutan

Los hooks se disparan en momentos precisos del ciclo de ejecuciÃ³n. Este es el orden cronolÃ³gico:

FASE 1: INICIALIZACIÃ“N
â‘  SessionInitialize â†’ Carga memoria y config
â‘¡ UserPromptSubmit Hook â†’ Inyecta contexto automÃ¡tico
FASE 2: EJECUCIÃ“N
â‘¢ PreToolUse Hook â†’ Valida, checa costos, aplica rate limits
â‘£ Tool ejecuta â†’ Bash, Read, Edit, etc
â‘¤ PostToolUse Hook â†’ Formatea, testa, monitorea
FASE 3: ERROR/RECUPERACIÃ“N
â‘¥ OnToolError Hook (si falla) â†’ RecuperaciÃ³n automÃ¡tica
FASE 4: LIMPIEZA
â‘¦ SessionCleanup â†’ Guarda estado, logging, exit

Hook 1: Security Audit (PreToolUse)

Valida cada acciÃ³n antes de ejecutarla â€” bloquea comandos peligrosos y previene exposiciÃ³n de credenciales.

ConfiguraciÃ³n: .claude.json
{
  "hooks": {
    "PreToolUse": {
      "enabled": true,
      "rules": [
        {
          "name": "Block dangerous commands",
          "pattern": "(rm -rf|eval|curl.*\\||sh -c|sudo)",
          "action": "block",
          "message": "Comando potencialmente peligroso."
        },
        {
          "name": "Block PII exposure",
          "pattern": "(password|token|secret|apiKey|API_KEY)\\s*=",
          "action": "block",
          "message": "No se permite escribir credenciales."
        },
        {
          "name": "Block SQL injection patterns",
          "pattern": "(DROP|TRUNCATE|DELETE)\\s+FROM\\s+(users|auth|payments)",
          "action": "block",
          "message": "OperaciÃ³n destructiva bloqueada."
        }
      ]
    }
  }
}
Flujo de EjecuciÃ³n
Claude intenta ejecutar: rm -rf /data
    â†“
PreToolUse hook intercepts
    â†“
Checa contra pattern: "(rm -rf|...)"
    â†“
MATCH encontrada âœ—
    â†“
AcciÃ³n: "block"
    â†“
Respuesta: âŒ "Comando potencialmente peligroso."
    â†“
Tool NO se ejecuta, usuario informado

Hook 2: Auto-Format + Tests (PostToolUse)

DespuÃ©s de escribir cÃ³digo, formatea automÃ¡ticamente y corre tests. Garantiza calidad sin intervenciÃ³n manual.

ConfiguraciÃ³n: .claude.json
{
  "hooks": {
    "PostToolUse": {
      "enabled": true,
      "sequence": [
        {
          "name": "Format code",
          "command": "npm run format",
          "onError": "warn",
          "timeout": "30s"
        },
        {
          "name": "Lint check",
          "command": "npm run lint",
          "onError": "warn",
          "timeout": "30s"
        },
        {
          "name": "Run tests",
          "command": "npm test -- --watch=false",
          "onError": "abort",
          "timeout": "120s"
        }
      ]
    }
  }
}

Hook 3: Context Injection (UserPromptSubmit)

Inyecta automÃ¡ticamente contexto relevante (git status, memory, env) antes de procesar cada prompt del usuario.

ConfiguraciÃ³n: .claude.json
{
  "hooks": {
    "UserPromptSubmit": {
      "enabled": true,
      "injections": [
        {
          "name": "Git status",

Hook 4: Cost Warning (PreToolUse)

Hook 7: Tool Error Recovery (OnToolError)

          "name": "Bash timeout",

            { "action": "retry", "times": 2, "backoff": "exponential" },
            { "action": "notify", "message": "âš ï¸ Command failed" }
          ]

        {
          "name": "File not found",

ðŸ›¡ï¸ Error Handling: Â¿QuÃ© pasa si un Hook Falla?

Estrategias de recuperaciÃ³n cuando hooks encuentran problemas:

Escenario 1: PreToolUse hook se crashea
â†’ onHookError: "warn" â†’ Hook skipped, tool ejecuta normalmente
Escenario 2: SessionInitialize falla (required: true)
â†’ onError: "abort" â†’ Claude Code NO inicia, debe fijar el error
ConfiguraciÃ³n: onHookError vs onError
onHookError = si hook se crashea | onError = si acciÃ³n en hook falla

Caso 1: Code Review AutomÃ¡tico vÃ­a GitHub MCP

Dificultad: FÃ¡cil | Setup: 30 minutos | Ahorros: 5 horas/semana

Objetivo

Automatizar la revisiÃ³n de cÃ³digo en GitHub PRs. Cada PR nueva se revisa automÃ¡ticamente con feedback detallado sobre calidad, seguridad y mejoras, eliminando la necesidad de reviews manuales.

Beneficio Esperado

  • 5 horas/semana ahorradas: 15 PRs/semana Ã— 20 min auto vs 30 min manual
  • Feedback consistente: Sin variaciÃ³n por revisor, siempre criterios iguales
  • CI/CD mÃ¡s rÃ¡pido: Reviews se completan mientras haces otra cosa
  • Mejora de calidad: Catches edge cases y patrones antes de merge

Requisitos Previos

  • Repositorio pÃºblico o privado en GitHub
  • GitHub Personal Access Token (PAT) con scopes: repo, workflow
  • GitHub Actions habilitado en el repo
  • MCP: github (oficial de Anthropic)

Pasos Detallados

# 1. Ve a GitHub â†’ Settings â†’ Developer settings â†’ Personal access tokens â†’ Tokens (classic)
# 2. Click "Generate new token (classic)"
# 3. Token name: "Claude Code Reviews"
# 4. Expiration: 90 days (rotaciÃ³n recomendada)
# 5. Scopes necesarios:
    - repo (completo): read/write repos, workflows, issues, PRs
    - workflow: acceso a GitHub Actions
    - gist: opcional si necesitas crear snippets
# 6. Copia el token: ghp_xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx
# 7. GUARDAR SEGURO: Solo se muestra una vez
# Setup con environment variable
export GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN=ghp_xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

# O agregarlo a .bashrc/.zshrc para persistir
echo 'export GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN=ghp_xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx' >> ~/.bashrc
source ~/.bashrc

# Verificar conexiÃ³n
curl -H "Authorization: token ghp_xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx" \
  https://api.github.com/user \
  -H "Accept: application/vnd.github.v3+json"

# Output esperado:
# {
#   "login": "tu-usuario",
#   "id": 12345,
#   "repos_url": "https://api.github.com/user/repos",
#   ...
# }
name: Claude Code Review
on:
  pull_request:
    types: [opened, synchronize]
    paths:
      - '**.js'
      - '**.ts'
      - '**.tsx'
      - '**.py'
      - '**.rs'

jobs:
  review:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      pull-requests: write
      contents: read
    steps:
      - name: Checkout code
        uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0

      - name: Get PR diff
        id: diff
        run: |
          git fetch origin pull/${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}/head:pr-branch
          git diff origin/main...pr-branch > /tmp/changes.diff
          cat /tmp/changes.diff

      - name: Run Claude Code Review
        id: review
        env:
          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
          GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN }}
          PR_NUMBER: ${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}
          REPO: ${{ github.repository }}
        run: |
          # Usar Claude Code CLI directamente
          claude --no-input "
          Eres un code reviewer experto. Analiza estos cambios y proporciona:
          1. Problemas crÃ­ticos (seguridad, bugs)
          2. Mejoras de cÃ³digo (patrones, performance)
          3. Testing: Â¿hay tests nuevos? Â¿suficientes?
          4. DocumentaciÃ³n: Â¿estÃ¡ actualizada?
          5. PuntuaciÃ³n general: APROBADO / CAMBIOS_MENORES / RECHAZA

          Cambios:
          \`\`\`diff
          $(cat /tmp/changes.diff)
          \`\`\`

          Responde en formato JSON para que GitHub lo procese.
          " > /tmp/review.json

      - name: Post review comment
        uses: actions/github-script@v7
        with:
          script: |
            const fs = require('fs');
            const review = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync('/tmp/review.json', 'utf8'));

            github.rest.pulls.createReview({
              owner: context.repo.owner,
              repo: context.repo.repo,
              pull_number: context.issue.number,
              event: review.decision === 'APROBADO' ? 'APPROVE' : 'REQUEST_CHANGES',
              body: `## Claude Code Review\n\n${review.summary}\n\n**Issues:** ${review.issues.length}\n**Improvements:** ${review.improvements.length}`
            });

ValidaciÃ³n

  1. Abre una PR de prueba (cambio pequeÃ±o)
  2. Verifica que GitHub Actions corra automÃ¡ticamente
  3. Espera review de Claude como comentario en PR
  4. Verifica que el review contenga: Issues, Improvements, Score
  5. Prueba modificar cÃ³digo segÃºn feedback y hacer push (rereview automÃ¡tico)

MÃ©tricas de Ã‰xito

  • âœ… 100% de PRs revieweadas dentro de 5 minutos de abrir
  • âœ… Feedback detallado (3+ puntos por review)
  • âœ… 0 false positives (feedback siempre relevante)
  • âœ… Review time < 5 min (vs 30 min manual)

Gotchas y Soluciones

Problema: GitHub Actions timeout despuÃ©s de 6 horas

SoluciÃ³n: Para diffs grandes (>50MB), splitea en chunks o usa git diff --cached

Problema: Claude review es genÃ©rico / no entiende contexto del proyecto

SoluciÃ³n: AÃ±ade contexto en el prompt: "Lee CLAUDE.md del repo para arquitectura y patrones antes de revisar"

Pro tip: Configura reglas de protecciÃ³n de rama: "Requiere revisiÃ³n de Claude APPROVE antes de merge"

IntegraciÃ³n con Workflow Existente

Combina con: Branch protection rules + Status checks + CODEOWNERS en .github/CODEOWNERS para que humans revisen solo PRs que Claude marque como riesgosas.

Caso 2: CI/CD Risk Analysis â€” Post-Merge Safety Gates

Dificultad: Media | Setup: 45 minutos | Ahorros: 3+ horas/semana

Objetivo

Ejecutar anÃ¡lisis de riesgo automÃ¡tico en CADA MERGE a main. Claude identifica cambios peligrosos (auth, database, sin tests) y alerta por Slack con severidad y acciones recomendadas.

Beneficio Esperado

  • PrevenciÃ³n de bugs en producciÃ³n: Catches cambios riesgosos antes de deployment
  • Alertas en tiempo real: Slack notification dentro de 2 minutos del merge
  • Decisiones informadas: Equipo sabe quÃ© riesgo tiene cada merge
  • AuditorÃ­a automÃ¡tica: Historial completo de cambios riesgosos

Requisitos Previos

  • Repository en GitHub/GitLab con acceso a main branch
  • Slack workspace + bot token
  • MCPs: github + slack
  • GitHub Actions o CI/CD runner disponible

Pasos Detallados

# 1. Ve a https://api.slack.com/apps
# 2. Click "Create New App" â†’ "From scratch"
# 3. App name: "Claude Risk Alert Bot"
# 4. Workspace: Selecciona tu workspace
# 5. Left menu â†’ OAuth & Permissions
# 6. Scopes (add these):
    - chat:write (enviar mensajes)
    - chat:write.public (en canales pÃºblicos)
    - files:write (para compartir diffs)
    - reactions:write (para aÃ±adir emojis)
# 7. Install to Workspace
# 8. Copy Bot Token: xoxb_xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx
# 9. Left menu â†’ Event Subscriptions
#    Enable Events ON
#    Subscribe to bot events:
      - app_mention
      - message.im
# 10. Save changes
name: Claude Risk Analysis Post-Merge
on:
  push:
    branches:
      - main
    paths:
      - '**.js'
      - '**.ts'
      - '**.py'
      - '**.sql'
      - '**.yml'
      - 'Dockerfile'
      - 'docker-compose.yml'
      - 'package.json'
      - 'requirements.txt'

jobs:
  risk_analysis:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - name: Checkout
        uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 10

      - name: Get last commit diff
        id: diff
        run: |
          git show HEAD --format="" --name-only > /tmp/files.txt
          git show HEAD > /tmp/commit.patch
          echo "FILES_CHANGED=$(cat /tmp/files.txt | wc -l)" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT

      - name: Analyze Risk with Claude
        id: risk
        env:
          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
        run: |
          claude --no-input "
          Analiza este commit MERGE de seguridad:

          Archivos modificados:
          \`\`\`
          $(cat /tmp/files.txt)
          \`\`\`

          Cambios:
          \`\`\`patch
          $(head -100 /tmp/commit.patch)  # Primeros 100 lÃ­neas
          \`\`\`

          EvalÃºa RIESGO en escala HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW:
          - Â¿Cambios en auth, database, or infrastructure?
          - Â¿Hay tests nuevos covering los cambios?
          - Â¿Dependencies actualizadas? Â¿breaking changes?
          - Â¿Secrets o credentials hardcodeadas?
          - Â¿Performance o security implications?

          Responde JSON:
          {
            \"severity\": \"HIGH|MEDIUM|LOW\",
            \"summary\": \"Una lÃ­nea resumen\",
            \"risks\": [\"risk 1\", \"risk 2\"],
            \"recommendations\": [\"action 1\", \"action 2\"],
            \"affected_areas\": [\"auth\", \"database\", etc]
          }
          " > /tmp/risk.json

      - name: Post Slack Alert
        uses: 8398a7/action-slack@v3
        with:
          status: custom
          payload: |
            {
              "attachments": [
                {
                  "color": "${{ fromJson(steps.risk.outputs.risk).severity == 'HIGH' && 'danger' || 'warning' }}",
                  "title": "${{ fromJson(steps.risk.outputs.risk).severity }} Risk: ${{ github.event.head_commit.message }}",
                  "text": "${{ fromJson(steps.risk.outputs.risk).summary }}",
                  "fields": [
                    {
                      "title": "Risks",
                      "value": "${{ join(fromJson(steps.risk.outputs.risk).risks, '\n') }}",
                      "short": false
                    },
                    {
                      "title": "Recommended Actions",
                      "value": "${{ join(fromJson(steps.risk.outputs.risk).recommendations, '\n') }}",
                      "short": false
                    },
                    {
                      "title": "Files Changed",
                      "value": "${{ steps.diff.outputs.FILES_CHANGED }}",
                      "short": true
                    },
                    {
                      "title": "Author",
                      "value": "${{ github.event.head_commit.author.name }}",
                      "short": true
                    }
                  ],
                  "footer": "Claude Risk Analysis â€¢ ${{ github.repository }}",
                  "ts": ${{ github.event.head_commit.timestamp }}
                }
              ]
            }
        env:
          SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL: ${{ secrets.SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL }}

ValidaciÃ³n

  1. Haz merge de una PR a main (test branch)
  2. Verifica que GitHub Actions ejecute el workflow dentro de 1 minuto
  3. Verifica Slack message en #deployments o canal configurado
  4. Mensaje debe incluir: Severity badge, summary, risks, recommendations
  5. Prueba con cambio "risky" (auth modification) â†’ debe ser HIGH

MÃ©tricas de Ã‰xito

  • âœ… 100% de merges analizados dentro de 2 min
  • âœ… HIGH risk cambios correctamente identificados
  • âœ… Equipo actÃºa dentro de 30 min en HIGH risk alerts
  • âœ… 0 bugs en producciÃ³n relacionados a cambios que Claude podrÃ­a haber detectado

Gotchas y Soluciones

Problema: Muchos false positives â†’ equipo ignora alertas

SoluciÃ³n: AÃ±ade context: "Ignore library updates que pasen tests. Focus: auth, database, security changes"

Problema: Slack messages muy largas

SoluciÃ³n: Limita a top 3 risks. Para anÃ¡lisis completo, linkea a GitHub Actions logs

IntegraciÃ³n con Workflow

Combina con: GitOps deployment gates. Si risk == HIGH, requiere approval manual antes de deploy a producciÃ³n. CloudFormation change sets, Terraform plans, etc.

Caso 3: Team Context Sharing â€” Onboarding AutomÃ¡tico

Dificultad: Media | Setup: 20 minutos | Ahorros: 2-3 horas/onboarding

Objetivo

Centralizar toda arquitectura, decisiones de diseÃ±o y bugs histÃ³ricos en CLAUDE.md. Nuevos developers leen automÃ¡ticamente el contexto antes de cualquier pregunta.

Beneficio Esperado

  • Onboarding en 15 minutos: vs 2-3 horas de explicaciÃ³n manual
  • Evitar re-explicar: Decisiones arquitectÃ³nicas documentadas
  • Consistencia: Todos entienden por quÃ© ciertas decisiones
  • Referencia viva: Se actualiza con el cÃ³digo, no se vuelve stale

Requisitos Previos

  • Acceso write al repo (CLAUDE.md en raÃ­z)

Pasos Detallados

# CLAUDE.md - Project Context & Architecture

## Project Overview
**Project:** [Your Project Name]
**Stack:** Node.js + TypeScript + React + PostgreSQL
**Repository:** github.com/your-org/project
**Team:** 5 engineers, 1 tech lead

---

## Architecture at a Glance

```
â”Œâ”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”     â”Œâ”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”     â”Œâ”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”
â”‚   React UI  â”‚â”€â”€â”€â”€â†’â”‚  API Gateway â”‚â”€â”€â”€â”€â†’â”‚ PostgreSQL  â”‚
â”‚  (Next.js)  â”‚     â”‚   (Node.js)  â”‚     â”‚  + Redis    â”‚
â””â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”˜     â””â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”˜     â””â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”˜
      â†“                    â†“
  Auth (OAuth2)    Middleware (Logger,
                   Auth, Rate Limit)
```

### Core Components
1. **Frontend (React/Next.js)**
   - Pages: /dashboard, /projects, /settings
   - State: Redux (global) + hooks (local)
   - Auth: OAuth2 via Auth0

2. **Backend (Node.js/Express)**
   - REST API at /api/v1/*
   - Authentication: JWT tokens (24h expiry)
   - Rate limiting: 100 req/min per IP

3. **Database (PostgreSQL)**
   - Primary: AWS RDS (us-east-1)
   - Replica: Read-only for analytics
   - Migrations: db/migrations/* via Knex

---

## Design Decisions & Why

### Decision: Why PostgreSQL over MongoDB?
**Date:** 2023-01-15 | **Status:** Active
**Reasoning:**
- Strong consistency required for financial transactions
- Complex joins for reporting
- ACID compliance non-negotiable
- Schema validation prevents data corruption

**Alternative Considered:** MongoDB
**Why rejected:** Weak consistency, ORM overhead

### Decision: Why Redux over Context API?
**Date:** 2023-03-20 | **Status:** Active
**Reasoning:**
- Devtools for time-travel debugging
- Middleware system for side effects (thunks)
- Multiple slices = better code organization

**Learning:** Context API fine for small apps, Redux scales

### Decision: OAuth2 instead of basic auth?
**Date:** 2023-02-01 | **Status:** Active
**Reasoning:**
- Users can have multiple identities (Google, GitHub)
- No password management liability
- Industry standard

---

## Historical Bugs & Lessons Learned

### Bug: Race Condition in Auth Flow
**Date Discovered:** 2023-06-14 | **Severity:** CRITICAL
**Symptom:** Users sometimes logged out unexpectedly
**Root Cause:** Two concurrent requests modifying `localStorage` + Redux state simultaneously
**Solution:** Introduced mutex lock in auth reducer using `redux-persist` lock
**Prevention:** Always sanitize async state updates with `immer` to detect mutations

**Code Reference:** `src/redux/authSlice.ts` line 45

```typescript
// BAD: Direct mutation
state.user = action.payload;

// GOOD: Immer-safe
return { ...state, user: action.payload };
```

### Bug: N+1 Query in /api/projects
**Date Discovered:** 2023-07-02 | **Severity:** MEDIUM
**Symptom:** API response time 5s+ when fetching 100 projects
**Root Cause:** Loop fetching user for each project: `projects.forEach(p => getUser(p.userId))`
**Solution:** Use SQL JOIN + aggregation in single query
**Prevention:** Use DataLoader pattern or batch queries

**Code Reference:** `src/services/projectService.ts` line 78

## Deployment & DevOps

### Environments
- **Development:** localhost:3000 (Webpack dev server)
- **Staging:** staging.yourapp.com (AWS Lambda)
- **Production:** yourapp.com (AWS Lambda + CloudFront)

### CI/CD Pipeline
```
Push to GitHub â†’ GitHub Actions (lint, test, build)
   â†“
Merge to main â†’ Deploy to staging
   â†“
Manual approval â†’ Deploy to production (blue/green)
```

### Monitoring
- **Errors:** Sentry (real-time alerts to Slack #errors)
- **Performance:** DataDog (dashboards for response time, error rates)
- **Logs:** CloudWatch (AWS Lambda logs)

## Getting Started (First Day)

1. **Clone & setup:**
   ```bash
   git clone https://github.com/your-org/project
   cd project
   npm install
   npm run dev
   ```

2. **Create `.env.local` (ask tech lead for values):**
   ```
   REACT_APP_API_URL=http://localhost:3001
   REACT_APP_AUTH0_DOMAIN=your-auth0.auth0.com
   REACT_APP_AUTH0_CLIENT_ID=xxxxx
   ```

3. **Run migrations:**
   ```bash
   npm run db:migrate
   npm run db:seed  # Test data
   ```

4. **Start dev server:**
   ```bash
   npm run dev
   # Frontend: http://localhost:3000
   # Backend: http://localhost:3001
   ```

5. **Ask in Slack:** @tech-lead if anything breaks

|------|---------|-----------|
| `src/redux/authSlice.ts` | Auth state logic | Every login/logout |
| `src/services/projectService.ts` | Project CRUD operations | Feature work |
| `db/migrations/*` | Database schema changes | New columns |
| `src/ui/pages/Dashboard.tsx` | Main landing page | UI changes |
| `package.json` | Dependencies & scripts | Adding packages |

## Testing Strategy

- **Unit Tests:** Jest for services & utils
- **Integration Tests:** Jest + @testing-library/react for components
- **E2E Tests:** Cypress for critical flows (auth, payment)

```bash
npm run test           # Jest (watch mode)
npm run test:e2e       # Cypress (interactive)
npm run test:coverage  # Coverage report
```

**Requirement:** >80% coverage for services, >60% for UI

---

## Common Tasks

### "I need to add a new API endpoint"
1. Create route in `src/api/yourFeature.ts`
2. Create service method in `src/services/yourFeatureService.ts`
3. Add Redux slice in `src/redux/yourFeatureSlice.ts` (if global state)
4. Test with Jest
5. Add UI component to call it
6. Update docs (this file) if architectural change

### "The app is slow"
1. Check DataDog dashboard for bottleneck
2. Profile with Chrome DevTools (Frontend) or NODE --prof (Backend)
3. Usually: N+1 queries or unnecessary re-renders
4. See "N+1 Query Bug" above for solution

### "Tests are failing"
```bash
npm run test -- --watch src/services  # Run specific test
npm run test -- --updateSnapshot      # Update snapshots if intentional
```

Caso 4: Multi-MCP Integration â€” GitHub + Postgres + Slack Workflow

Dificultad: Avanzada | Setup: 2-3 horas | Ahorros: 15+ horas/semana

Objetivo

Orquestar un workflow complejo: GitHub MCP lee PRs â†’ Postgres MCP valida datos â†’ Slack MCP notifica al equipo, todo en una sesiÃ³n automÃ¡tica sin cÃ³digo.

Beneficio Esperado

  • 80% automatizaciÃ³n: Tasks que requerÃ­an manual coordination ahora automÃ¡ticas
  • Zero context switching: Un agente orquestra mÃºltiples herramientas
  • Faster time-to-resolution: Alerts y acciones se distribuyen en segundos
  • Data consistency: Single source of truth via MCPs

Requisitos Previos

  • MCPs: github, postgres (o mysql/sqlite), slack
  • Credentials para cada servicio
  • Claude Agent SDK o Claude CLI con multi-tool support
  • PostgreSQL database con acceso de escritura

Pasos Detallados

# Instalar MCPs
claude mcp add github \
  --env GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN=ghp_xxxxx

claude mcp add postgres \
  --env DATABASE_URL=postgresql://user:pass@localhost:5432/mydb

claude mcp add slack \
  --env SLACK_BOT_TOKEN=xoxb_xxxxx

# Verificar que estÃ¡n listos
claude mcp list
# Output:
# âœ“ github (connected)
# âœ“ postgres (connected)
# âœ“ slack (connected)
-- Tabla para tracking de PRs procesados
CREATE TABLE pr_validations (
  id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  pr_number INT NOT NULL,
  repository VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
  author VARCHAR(255),
  title VARCHAR(512),
  status VARCHAR(50) DEFAULT 'pending',  -- pending, approved, flagged, merged
  risk_level VARCHAR(20),  -- LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH
  validation_date TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW(),
  notes TEXT
);

-- Tabla para datos que se han validado (cualquier dato crÃ­tico)
CREATE TABLE data_validations (
  id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  object_type VARCHAR(100),  -- 'user', 'transaction', 'config'
  object_id VARCHAR(255),
  validation_result VARCHAR(20),  -- PASS, FAIL, WARNING
  details JSONB,
  validated_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW()
);

-- Ãndices para query rÃ¡pido
CREATE INDEX idx_pr_number ON pr_validations(pr_number);
CREATE INDEX idx_status ON pr_validations(status);
CREATE INDEX idx_validated_at ON data_validations(validated_at DESC);
import Anthropic from "@anthropic-ai/sdk";

const client = new Anthropic();

// Define tools que Claude puede usar
const tools: Anthropic.Tool[] = [
  {
    name: "github_get_pr",
    description: "Obtener detalles de un PR en GitHub",
    input_schema: {
      type: "object",
      properties: {
        repo: { type: "string", description: "owner/repo" },
        pr_number: { type: "number" }
      },
      required: ["repo", "pr_number"]
    }
  },
  {
    name: "postgres_query",
    description: "Ejecutar query en PostgreSQL",
    input_schema: {
      type: "object",
      properties: {
        query: { type: "string", description: "SQL query" }
      },
      required: ["query"]
    }
  },
  {
    name: "slack_send_message",
    description: "Enviar mensaje a Slack",
    input_schema: {
      type: "object",
      properties: {
        channel: { type: "string" },
        text: { type: "string" }
      },
      required: ["channel", "text"]
    }
  }
];

async function processGitHubPR(repo: string, prNumber: number) {
  const messages: Anthropic.MessageParam[] = [
    {
      role: "user",
      content: `
      Procesa este PR automÃ¡ticamente:
      - Repository: ${repo}
      - PR Number: ${prNumber}

      Workflow:
      1. ObtÃ©n detalles del PR con github_get_pr
      2. Valida los cambios en PostgreSQL: Â¿hay archivos riesgosos?
         Inserta validaciÃ³n en tabla data_validations
      3. Si validaciÃ³n = PASS:
         - EnvÃ­a mensaje Slack: âœ… PR ${prNumber} validated
      4. Si validaciÃ³n = FAIL:
         - EnvÃ­a mensaje Slack: âš ï¸ PR ${prNumber} flagged for review
      5. Actualiza tabla pr_validations con el resultado

      SÃ© especÃ­fico: Incluye quÃ© archivos fueron validados.
      `
    }
  ];

  let response = await client.messages.create({
    model: "claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022",
    max_tokens: 4096,
    tools: tools,
    messages: messages
  });

  // Agentic loop: ejecuta mientras Claude pida tools
  while (response.stop_reason === "tool_use") {
    const toolUseBlock = response.content.find(
      (block): block is Anthropic.ToolUseBlock => block.type === "tool_use"
    );

    if (!toolUseBlock) break;

    // Simular respuestas de tools (en prod, llamarÃ­as APIs reales)
    let toolResult = "";
    if (toolUseBlock.name === "github_get_pr") {
      // Mock response
      toolResult = JSON.stringify({
        title: "feat: add authentication",
        author: "john-doe",
        files_changed: ["src/auth.ts", "src/api.ts"],
        additions: 150,
        deletions: 30,
        status: "open"
      });
    } else if (toolUseBlock.name === "postgres_query") {
      // Mock response
      toolResult = JSON.stringify({
        affected_rows: 1,
        status: "success"
      });
    } else if (toolUseBlock.name === "slack_send_message") {
      // Mock response
      toolResult = JSON.stringify({
        ts: "1234567890.123456",
        status: "ok"
      });
    }

    // ContinÃºa conversaciÃ³n
    messages.push({
      role: "assistant",
      content: response.content
    });

    messages.push({
      role: "user",
      content: [
        {
          type: "tool_result",
          tool_use_id: toolUseBlock.id,
          content: toolResult
        }
      ]
    });

    response = await client.messages.create({
      model: "claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022",
      max_tokens: 4096,
      tools: tools,
      messages: messages
    });
  }

  // Extrae respuesta final
  const finalText = response.content
    .filter((block): block is Anthropic.TextBlock => block.type === "text")
    .map((block) => block.text)
    .join("\n");

  console.log("Workflow completed:", finalText);
}

// Trigger: cuando hay PR nueva
processGitHubPR("your-org/your-repo", 42);

ValidaciÃ³n

  1. Abre un PR de prueba en tu repo
  2. Ejecuta: `npx ts-node orchestrate-workflow.ts`
  3. Verifica logs: "Workflow completed: Validation = PASS"
  4. Verifica PostgreSQL: INSERT en pr_validations completado
  5. Verifica Slack: Mensaje en #pr-validations con resultado
  6. Prueba con cambio riesgoso (auth file) â†’ debe ser FAIL

MÃ©tricas de Ã‰xito

  • âœ… 100% de PRs procesadas automÃ¡ticamente
  • âœ… Slack notification dentro de 5 minutos de abrir PR
  • âœ… PostgreSQL audit trail completo para cada PR
  • âœ… Zero manual coordination needed (vs 3-4h/dÃ­a antes)

Gotchas y Soluciones

Problema: MCPs timeout si workflow es larga

SoluciÃ³n: Divide en sub-workflows. MCP 1 (GitHub), MCP 2 (Postgres), MCP 3 (Slack) en secuencia, no en paralelo

Problema: Credentials se exponen en logs

SoluciÃ³n: Usa env variables, nunca logues tokens. Review logs regularmente.

IntegraciÃ³n

Combina con: GitHub Actions trigger + CloudFunction/Lambda para ejecutar el orchestrator automÃ¡ticamente en cada PR.

Caso 5: Automated Documentation Generation â€” Code to Docs

Dificultad: Avanzada | Setup: 1-2 horas | Ahorros: 8 horas/semana

Objetivo

Generar documentaciÃ³n automÃ¡tica cada vez que el cÃ³digo cambia. APIs docs, guides, examples siempre sincronizados con el cÃ³digo actual.

Beneficio Esperado

  • 8 horas/semana ahorradas: Docs generation + reviews + updates automÃ¡ticas
  • Docs siempre actualizadas: Cambio cÃ³digo â†’ Docs se regeneran â†’ PR automÃ¡tico
  • Mejor developer experience: Ejemplos siempre funcionales
  • Menos onboarding time: Docs comprehensive desde el inicio

Requisitos Previos

  • Codebase con TypeScript/JSDoc o Python docstrings
  • GitHub repo con acceso write
  • Claude Agent SDK + MCP github
  • Automated testing del cÃ³digo (para generar ejemplos)

Pasos Detallados

import Anthropic from "@anthropic-ai/sdk";
import fs from "fs";
import path from "path";

interface DocumentationArtifact {
  filename: string;
  content: string;
  type: "api" | "guide" | "example" | "troubleshooting";
}

const client = new Anthropic();

async function generateDocumentation(codeDir: string): Promise {
  // 1. Leer cÃ³digo fuente
  const sourceFiles = readSourceFiles(codeDir);

  // 2. Crear mensaje para Claude
  const response = await client.messages.create({
    model: "claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022",
    max_tokens: 8000,
    messages: [
      {
        role: "user",
        content: `
        Eres un experto documentador. Analiza este cÃ³digo y genera documentaciÃ³n:

        ${sourceFiles.map(f => \`
        FILE: \${f.name}
        \`\`\`\${f.ext}
        \${f.content}
        \`\`\`
        \`).join('\n')}

        Genera 4 archivos de documentaciÃ³n en formato JSON:

        {
          "api-reference.md": "DocumentaciÃ³n de API (functions, parameters, return types)",
          "getting-started.md": "GuÃ­a rÃ¡pida de inicio",
          "examples.md": "Ejemplos de cÃ³digo funcionales",
          "troubleshooting.md": "Problemas comunes y soluciones"
        }

        Requisitos:
        - API reference: Para cada funciÃ³n, incluye signature, description, parÃ¡metros, retorno
        - Examples: CÃ³digo real que funciona (basado en tests)
        - Markdown bien formateado
        - Include  blocks cuando sea relevante
        `
      }
    ]
  });

  // 3. Parse respuesta
  const textContent = response.content
    .filter((block): block is Anthropic.TextBlock => block.type === "text")
    .map(block => block.text)
    .join("");

  // Extract JSON from response
  const jsonMatch = textContent.match(/\{[\s\S]*\}/);
  if (!jsonMatch) throw new Error("Could not parse documentation JSON");

  const docs = JSON.parse(jsonMatch[0]);

  // 4. Convertir a artifacts
  const artifacts: DocumentationArtifact[] = [
    { filename: "API-REFERENCE.md", content: docs["api-reference.md"], type: "api" },
    { filename: "GETTING-STARTED.md", content: docs["getting-started.md"], type: "guide" },
    { filename: "EXAMPLES.md", content: docs["examples.md"], type: "example" },
    { filename: "TROUBLESHOOTING.md", content: docs["troubleshooting.md"], type: "troubleshooting" }
  ];

  return artifacts;
}

function readSourceFiles(dir: string) {
  const files = [];
  const entries = fs.readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true });

  for (const entry of entries) {
    if (entry.isFile() && /\.(ts|tsx|js|jsx|py)$/.test(entry.name)) {
      const fullPath = path.join(dir, entry.name);
      const content = fs.readFileSync(fullPath, "utf-8");
      files.push({
        name: entry.name,
        ext: entry.name.split(".").pop(),
        content: content.slice(0, 3000) // First 3000 chars
      });
    } else if (entry.isDirectory() && !entry.name.startsWith(".")) {
      files.push(...readSourceFiles(path.join(dir, entry.name)));
    }
  }

  return files;
}

// Main execution
(async () => {
  const artifacts = await generateDocumentation("./src");

  // Escribe archivos
  const docsDir = "./docs/generated";
  fs.mkdirSync(docsDir, { recursive: true });

  for (const artifact of artifacts) {
    fs.writeFileSync(
      path.join(docsDir, artifact.filename),
      artifact.content
    );
    console.log(`âœ“ Generated ${artifact.filename}`);
  }
})();
name: Auto-Generate Documentation
on:
  push:
    branches:
      - main
    paths:
      - 'src/**/*.ts'
      - 'src/**/*.js'
      - 'README.md'
  workflow_dispatch:  # Manual trigger

jobs:
  generate:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: write
      pull-requests: write
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0

      - name: Setup Node
        uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: '18'

      - name: Install dependencies
        run: npm install

      - name: Generate Documentation
        run: npx ts-node doc-generator-agent.ts
        env:
          ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: ${{ secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY }}

      - name: Check if docs changed
        id: changed
        run: |
          if git diff --exit-code docs/generated/; then
            echo "changed=false" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
          else
            echo "changed=true" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
          fi

      - name: Create PR with updated docs
        if: steps.changed.outputs.changed == 'true'
        uses: peter-evans/create-pull-request@v5
        with:
          commit-message: 'docs: auto-generate documentation'
          title: '[AUTO] Documentation Updated'
          body: |
            This PR contains auto-generated documentation based on the latest code changes.

            - API Reference: docs/generated/API-REFERENCE.md
            - Getting Started: docs/generated/GETTING-STARTED.md
            - Examples: docs/generated/EXAMPLES.md
            - Troubleshooting: docs/generated/TROUBLESHOOTING.md

            Review and merge if everything looks good.
          branch: docs/auto-update
          labels: documentation, automated

ValidaciÃ³n

  1. Haz cambios al cÃ³digo (nuevo function, nueva clase)
  2. Push a main
  3. GitHub Actions dispara automÃ¡ticamente
  4. Espera 5-10 minutos
  5. Verifica PR automÃ¡tico creado con docs generadas
  6. Revisa que API-REFERENCE.md refleje el nuevo cÃ³digo
  7. Merge the PR

MÃ©tricas de Ã‰xito

  • âœ… 100% de cambios de cÃ³digo resultan en docs updated
  • âœ… Docs PR creado dentro de 10 min de push
  • âœ… 0 manual doc maintenance needed
  • âœ… Documentation always matches code (zero stale docs)

IntegraciÃ³n

Combina con: Docusaurus o ReadTheDocs para publicar docs automÃ¡ticamente. Trigger: PR merge â†’ docs publish.

ðŸ”´

Caso 6: Quality Gates in CI/CD â€” Coverage, Security, Performance

Dificultad: Avanzada | Setup: 1.5-2 horas | Ahorros: 4+ horas/semana

Objetivo

Implementar quality gates automÃ¡ticos que bloquean deployment si no cumplen criterios: test coverage 80%+, 0 high-severity security issues, performance baseline no degradado.

Beneficio Esperado

  • 4+ horas/semana ahorradas: Manual testing / security scanning eliminado
  • Zero bugs en producciÃ³n debidos a coverage insuficiente o vulnerabilidades conocidas
  • Performance consistency: Detecta regressions antes de deployment
  • Compliance automÃ¡tico: Siempre cumple estÃ¡ndares sin esfuerzo manual

Requisitos Previos

  • Testing suite completo (Jest, pytest, etc.)
  • Security scanner (npm audit, OWASP ZAP, Snyk)
  • Performance monitoring (Lighthouse, k6, Artillery)
  • GitHub Actions disponible

Pasos Detallados

name: Quality Gates - Pre-Deployment Checks
on:
  pull_request:
    types: [opened, synchronize]
  push:
    branches:
      - main
      - develop

jobs:
  test-coverage:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: '18'

      - run: npm install

      - name: Run Tests with Coverage
        run: npm run test:coverage -- --json --outputFile=coverage.json

      - name: Enforce Coverage Threshold
        run: |
          COVERAGE=$(jq '.total.lines.pct' coverage.json)
          if (( $(echo "$COVERAGE < 80" | bc -l) )); then
            echo "âŒ Coverage $COVERAGE% is below 80% threshold"
            exit 1
          fi
          echo "âœ… Coverage $COVERAGE% passes threshold"

      - name: Upload coverage to Codecov
        uses: codecov/codecov-action@v3
        with:
          files: ./coverage.json
          flags: unittests

  security-scan:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: '18'

      - name: Run npm audit
        run: |
          npm audit --json > audit.json || true

      - name: Check for high-severity vulnerabilities
        run: |
          HIGH_VULNS=$(jq '[.vulnerabilities[] | select(.severity == "high" or .severity == "critical")] | length' audit.json)
          if [ $HIGH_VULNS -gt 0 ]; then
            echo "âŒ Found $HIGH_VULNS high-severity vulnerabilities"
            jq '.vulnerabilities[] | select(.severity == "high" or .severity == "critical")' audit.json
            exit 1
          fi
          echo "âœ… No high-severity vulnerabilities found"

      - name: Snyk scan (optional, if configured)
        run: |
          npx snyk test --severity-threshold=high || echo "âš ï¸ Snyk issues (non-blocking)"

  performance-check:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
        with:
          node-version: '18'

      - run: npm install && npm run build

      - name: Lighthouse CI
        run: |
          npm install -g @lhci/cli@
          lhci autorun --config=lighthouserc.json || echo "âš ï¸ Performance check completed"

      - name: Baseline Comparison
        run: |
          # Compare bundle size
          CURRENT_SIZE=$(du -sb dist/ | awk '{print $1}')
          BASELINE_SIZE=$(cat .performance-baseline 2>/dev/null || echo $CURRENT_SIZE)

          INCREASE_PCT=$((($CURRENT_SIZE - $BASELINE_SIZE) * 100 / $BASELINE_SIZE))

          if [ $INCREASE_PCT -gt 10 ]; then
            echo "âš ï¸ Bundle size increased by ${INCREASE_PCT}%"
            echo "Previous: $BASELINE_SIZE bytes"
            echo "Current: $CURRENT_SIZE bytes"
            exit 1
          fi
          echo "âœ… Bundle size within baseline (+${INCREASE_PCT}%)"

  quality-gates-report:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    needs: [test-coverage, security-scan, performance-check]
    if: always()
    steps:
      - name: Determine Status
        run: |
          if [ "${{ needs.test-coverage.result }}" != "success" ]; then
            echo "âŒ Test coverage check failed"
            exit 1
          fi
          if [ "${{ needs.security-scan.result }}" != "success" ]; then
            echo "âŒ Security scan failed"
            exit 1
          fi
          if [ "${{ needs.performance-check.result }}" != "success" ]; then
            echo "âŒ Performance check failed"
            exit 1
          fi
          echo "âœ… All quality gates passed!"

      - name: Post Comment (PR only)
        if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'
        uses: actions/github-script@v7
        with:
          script: |
            github.rest.issues.createComment({
              issue_number: context.issue.number,
              owner: context.repo.owner,
              repo: context.repo.repo,
              body: '## âœ… Quality Gates Passed\n\n- âœ… Test Coverage: 80%+\n- âœ… Security: No high-severity issues\n- âœ… Performance: Within baseline\n\nReady to deploy!'
            })
{
  "ci": {
    "collect": {
      "staticDistDir": "./dist",
      "numberOfRuns": 3,
      "url": ["http://localhost:3000"]
    },
    "upload": {
      "target": "temporary-public-storage"
    },
    "assert": {
      "preset": "lighthouse:recommended",
      "assertions": {
        "categories:performance": ["error", { "minScore": 0.85 }],
        "categories:accessibility": ["error", { "minScore": 0.90 }],
        "categories:best-practices": ["error", { "minScore": 0.90 }],
        "categories:seo": ["error", { "minScore": 0.90 }]
      }
    }
  }
}

# Ejecutar una vez cuando el proyecto estÃ¡ en "buena" performance
du -sb dist/ | awk '{print $1}' > .performance-baseline

# Ver el baseline
cat .performance-baseline
# Output: 2048576 (bytes)

# Committear
git add .performance-baseline
git commit -m "perf: set performance baseline"
git push origin main

ValidaciÃ³n

  1. Abre una PR con cambios normales
  2. GitHub Actions ejecuta quality gates automÃ¡ticamente
  3. Verifica que todos los checks pasen (coverage, security, performance)
  4. Prueba con una PR que reduce coverage < 80% â†’ debe fallar
  5. Prueba agregando dependencia vulnerable â†’ debe fallar
  6. Verifica comentario automÃ¡tico en PR con resumen de gates

MÃ©tricas de Ã‰xito

  • âœ… 100% de PRs validadas contra quality gates
  • âœ… 0 merges a main si quality gates fallan
  • âœ… Coverage siempre 80%+ en producciÃ³n
  • âœ… 0 known vulnerabilities en dependencies
  • âœ… Performance nunca degrada >10% sin aprobaciÃ³n

Gotchas y Soluciones

Problema: False positives en security scan (fake vulnerabilities)

SoluciÃ³n: MantÃ©n whitelist de vulnerabilidades conocidas que no afectan. `npm audit --audit-level=moderate` para menos ruido

Problema: Performance check timeout / falla en CI environment

SoluciÃ³n: Usa URLs locales (http://localhost) en Lighthouse, no URLs externas. Almacena baseline en git.

Pro tip: Configura "soft gates" (warnings) vs "hard gates" (blocking). Security/coverage = hard. Performance = warning si <15% degradaciÃ³n.

IntegraciÃ³n

Combina con: Branch protection rules. Requiere que quality gates pasen antes de permitir merge. Status check: "Quality Gates Pass" requerido.

ProducciÃ³n Avanzada / Frameworks

ðŸ”— LangChain & CrewAI Integration

CuÃ¡ndo integrar Claude Code con frameworks existentes. Estrategias para conectar agentes de Claude como componentes en orquestaciones LangChain o CrewAI.

âš ï¸ DecisiÃ³n arquitectÃ³nica: Elige Claude Code puro si necesitas simplicidad y feedback humano. Usa LangChain/CrewAI solo si requieres orquestaciÃ³n compleja, multi-agente coordinada o integraciÃ³n con pipelines existentes.

Claude Code vs Frameworks: Matriz de DecisiÃ³n

Claude Code CLI (Puro)

  • Flujo interactivo developer-machine
  • Control manual paso a paso
  • Mejor para debugging y exploraciÃ³n
  • Menos overhead, mÃ¡s rÃ¡pido
  • Ideal: refactor, review, setup

LangChain (Recomendado)

  • OrquestaciÃ³n predefinida de agentes
  • IntegraciÃ³n con 200+ herramientas
  • Chain/RAG/Memory built-in
  • Ideal: pipelines de datos, RAG
  • Complejidad media, documentaciÃ³n excelente

CrewAI (Flexible)

  • Rol-basado multi-agente
  • Task-driven workflows
  • MÃ¡s flexible que LangChain
  • Ideal: equipos virtuales, equipos multidisciplinarios
  • Comunidad creciente, ideal experimental

IntegraciÃ³n LangChain: Claude Code como Agente

Setup Inicial

npm install langchain @langchain/anthropic @langchain/core
npm install -D @types/node

Agente Simple con Tool Use

import Anthropic from "@anthropic-ai/sdk";
import { Tool } from "@langchain/core/tools";
import { AgentExecutor, createToolCallingAgent } from "langchain/agents";
import { ChatPromptTemplate } from "@langchain/core/prompts";
import { ChatAnthropic } from "@langchain/anthropic";

// 1. Definir tools
const tools: Tool[] = [
  {
    name: "grep_codebase",
    description: "Busca patterns en el cÃ³digo fuente",
    schema: {
      type: "object",
      properties: {
        pattern: { type: "string" },
        path: { type: "string" }
      },
      required: ["pattern"]
    }
  },
  {
    name: "read_file",
    description: "Lee contenido de archivo",
    schema: {
      type: "object",
      properties: {
        path: { type: "string" }
      },
      required: ["path"]
    }
  }
];

// 2. Crear agente Claude
const model = new ChatAnthropic({
  modelName: "claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022",
  temperature: 0,
  apiKey: process.env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
});

const prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.fromMessages([
  ["system", "Eres un agente experto en cÃ³digo. Usa las tools disponibles."],
  ["human", "{input}"],
  ["ai", "{agent_scratchpad}"]
]);

const agent = createToolCallingAgent({ llm: model, tools, prompt });
const agentExecutor = new AgentExecutor({ agent, tools, verbose: true });

// 3. Ejecutar
const result = await agentExecutor.invoke({
  input: "Â¿CuÃ¡les son las funciones export en src/utils?"
});
console.log(result.output);

Agente con Memoria Persistente

import { BufferMemory } from "langchain/memory";
import { ConversationChain } from "langchain/chains";

const memory = new BufferMemory({
  memoryKey: "chat_history",
  inputKey: "input",
  outputKey: "output",
  returnMessages: true
});

const conversationChain = new ConversationChain({
  llm: model,
  memory: memory,
  prompt: ChatPromptTemplate.fromTemplate(
    `Eres un assistant de desarrollo. Contexto:
{chat_history}

Usuario: {input}
Assistant:`
  )
});

// MÃºltiples interacciones con memoria
await conversationChain.call({ input: "Mi proyecto es Node.js + TypeScript" });
await conversationChain.call({ input: "Â¿QuÃ© tests deberÃ­a agregar?" });
// Claude recordarÃ¡ que es Node.js + TS

IntegraciÃ³n CrewAI: Equipos Virtuales

Setup

pip install crewai crewai-tools
export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-xxxxx

Equipo Multi-Agente (Code Review)

from crewai import Agent, Task, Crew, Process
from crewai_tools import FileReadTool, DirectoryReadTool
from anthropic import Anthropic

# 1. Definir agentes (roles)
code_reviewer = Agent(
    role="Code Reviewer",
    goal="Revisar cambios de cÃ³digo para calidad y seguridad",
    backstory="Expert revisor con 15 aÃ±os de experiencia.",
    tools=[FileReadTool(), DirectoryReadTool()],
    verbose=True
)

security_specialist = Agent(
    role="Security Specialist",
    goal="Identificar vulnerabilidades de seguridad",
    backstory="Security expert certificado.",
    tools=[FileReadTool()],
    verbose=True
)

performance_analyst = Agent(
    role="Performance Analyst",
    goal="Detectar cuellos de botella y optimizaciones",
    backstory="Performance tuning specialist.",
    tools=[FileReadTool()],
    verbose=True
)

# 2. Definir tareas
review_task = Task(
    description="Revisar @{file_path}: estructura, claridad, testing",
    agent=code_reviewer,
    expected_output="Reporte detallado con issues y sugerencias"
)

security_task = Task(
    description="Analizar @{file_path} para vulnerabilidades comunes",
    agent=security_specialist,
    expected_output="Lista de riesgos de seguridad con severidad"
)

perf_task = Task(
    description="Analizar performance y proponer optimizaciones",
    agent=performance_analyst,
    expected_output="AnÃ¡lisis de complejidad + sugerencias"
)

# 3. Crear equipo
crew = Crew(
    agents=[code_reviewer, security_specialist, performance_analyst],
    tasks=[review_task, security_task, perf_task],
    process=Process.sequential,  # O Process.hierarchical
    verbose=True
)

# 4. Ejecutar
result = crew.kickoff(inputs={"file_path": "src/api/handlers.ts"})
print(result)

Best Practices & Patrones

âœ… CuÃ¡ndo usar Claude Code CLI puro

  • Desarrollo iterativo: Refactor, debug, exploraciones
  • Equipos pequeÃ±os: 1-3 developers en un repo
  • Feedback humano requerido: Decisiones complejas
  • Baja latencia: Necesitas respuestas inmediatas

âœ… CuÃ¡ndo usar LangChain

  • RAG (Retrieval Augmented Generation): DocumentaciÃ³n, PDFs
  • Chains predefinidas: Flujos secuenciales conocidos
  • IntegraciÃ³n con DBs: Vector stores, SQL databases
  • Production pipelines: 24/7 processing, batches

âœ… CuÃ¡ndo usar CrewAI

  • Multi-agente coordinado: Roles diferentes, dependencias
  • Equipos virtuales: Simular departamentos completos
  • Workflows complejos: Hierarchical, parallel, sequential
  • AutonomÃ­a alta: Agentes toman decisiones sin intervenciÃ³n

Ejemplo: Pipeline Hibrido (CLI + LangChain)

// 1. Developer inicia con Claude Code

// 2. Ejecutar tests automÃ¡ticos

// 3. Pipeline LangChain para anÃ¡lisis de impacto
const impactAnalysis = async (file: string) => {
  const tools = [
    { name: "grep", fn: async (p) => execSync(`grep -r "${p}" src/`).toString() },
    { name: "readFile", fn: async (p) => readFileSync(p, 'utf8') }
  ];

  const agent = await createToolCallingAgent({ llm: model, tools });
  const result = await agent.invoke({
    input: `Â¿QuÃ© archivos importan desde ${file}? AnÃ¡lisis de impacto.`
  });

  return result;
};

// 4. CrewAI para ticket + documentaciÃ³n automÃ¡tica
const crew = new Crew({
  agents: [docWriter, ticketCreator],
  tasks: [
    { agent: docWriter, description: "Actualizar docs del cambio" },
    { agent: ticketCreator, description: "Crear ticket con changelog" }
  ]
});
ProducciÃ³n Avanzada / Seguridad

ðŸ” Enterprise Security & Compliance

ðŸ”’ Seguridad crÃ­tica: En producciÃ³n, nunca hardcodes secrets. Usa variables de entorno, vaults (1Password, HashiCorp) o gestores nativos (AWS Secrets Manager).

1. Role-Based Access Control (RBAC)

Niveles de Permiso en Claude Code

{
  "version": "2.0",
  "permissions": {
    "global": {
      "read": ["*"],
      "write": [".claude/**", "docs/**"],
      "execute": ["npm", "node"]
    },
    "project": {
      "read": ["src/**", "package.json"],
      "write": ["src/**", "tests/**"],
      "execute": ["npm run test", "npm run build"],
      "deny": ["rm -rf /", "sudo *"]
    },
    "user": {
      "roles": ["developer", "reviewer"],
      "capabilities": ["edit", "test", "commit"]
    }
  },
  "audit": {
    "logAllChanges": true,
    "logDirectory": ".claude/audit-logs"
  }
}

DefiniciÃ³n de Roles Personalizados

roles:
  admin:
    description: "Control total"
    permissions:
      - read:*
      - write:*
      - execute:*
      - deploy
      - manage_users
      - view_audit_logs

  senior_engineer:
    description: "Development + Code Review"
    permissions:
      - read:*
      - write:src/**
      - write:tests/**
      - execute:npm
      - create_pr
      - approve_changes

  junior_engineer:
    description: "Development bajo supervisiÃ³n"
    permissions:
      - read:src/**
      - write:src/**
      - execute:npm run test
      - request_review

  security_auditor:
    description: "Solo lectura + auditorÃ­a"
    permissions:
      - read:*
      - view_audit_logs
      - view_permissions
      - report_vulnerabilities

users:
  alice@company.com:
    role: admin
    teams: [backend, platform]

  bob@company.com:
    role: senior_engineer
    teams: [backend]

  charlie@company.com:
    role: junior_engineer
    teams: [backend]
    requires_approval_for:
      - delete:*
      - deploy

ImplementaciÃ³n: Logging AutomÃ¡tico

import * as fs from 'fs';
import * as path from 'path';

class AuditLogger {
  private logDir = '.claude/audit-logs';

  logAction(action: AuditAction) {
    const today = new Date().toISOString().split('T')[0];
    const logFile = path.join(this.logDir, `${today}.jsonl`);

    const entry = {
      timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
      ...action,
      ipAddress: process.env.USER_IP,
      userAgent: process.env.CLAUDE_VERSION
    };

    fs.appendFileSync(logFile, JSON.stringify(entry) + '\n');

    // Enviar a sistema de logs centralizado
    if (process.env.LOG_SINK_URL) {
      fetch(process.env.LOG_SINK_URL, {
        method: 'POST',
        headers: { 'Authorization': `Bearer ${process.env.LOG_SINK_TOKEN}` },
        body: JSON.stringify(entry)
      }).catch(err => console.error('Log sink error:', err));
    }
  }

  queryLogs(filter: { userId?: string; action?: string; from?: Date }) {
    // Buscar logs filtrados para reportes de compliance
  }
}

export const auditLogger = new AuditLogger();

4. SOC2 & HIPAA Compliance Checklist

SOC2 Type II Requirements

  • CC6.1 Logical Access Controls: RBAC implementado, MFA habilitado
  • CC7.2 Audit Logging: Todos los cambios logged, retenidos 90 dÃ­as
  • CC9.1 Encryption: TLS 1.3 en trÃ¡nsito, AES-256 en reposo
  • A1.2 Availability: Uptime 99.95%, RTO < 4h, RPO < 1h
#!/bin/bash
# Ejecutar mensualmente
echo "SOC2 Compliance Check â€” $(date)"

# 1. Verificar RBAC
grep -c "role:" .claude/roles.yaml && echo "âœ… RBAC configured"

# 2. Verificar logs
find .claude/audit-logs -type f -mtime -30 | wc -l
echo "âœ… Recent audit logs found"

# 3. Verificar encriptaciÃ³n
openssl s_client -connect api.anthropic.com:443 -tls1_3 | grep "TLSv1.3"
echo "âœ… TLS 1.3 active"

# 4. Verificar MFA
ls ~/.claude/auth/mfa-enabled && echo "âœ… MFA enabled"

HIPAA Compliance (Healthcare)

  • PHI Handling: Nunca loguear datos de pacientes en plaintext
  • Encryption: AES-256 en BD, TLS en trÃ¡nsito
  • Access Controls: RBAC granular, auditorÃ­a de acceso a PHI
  • Breach Notification: Plan de respuesta < 24h
  • Business Associate Agreement: Requerido si usas terceros
// âŒ NUNCA hagas esto en HIPAA
console.log(`Paciente: ${patient.name}, SSN: ${patient.ssn}`);

// âœ… Haz esto
console.log(`Paciente ID: ${patient.id}`); // Solo ID desidentificado
auditLogger.logAccess({
  userId: 'doctor@hospital.com',
  resourceId: patient.id,
  action: 'view_medical_record'
});

// Encriptar datos sensibles
const encrypted = await encryptPHI(patient.ssn, process.env.HIPAA_KEY);
await db.patients.update(patient.id, { ssn: encrypted });

1Password Integration

#!/bin/bash
# Usar op CLI para acceder a secretos

export DB_PASSWORD=$(op read "op://Production/Database/password")

6. ComunicaciÃ³n Segura: TLS & API Keys

VerificaciÃ³n de Certificados TLS

import https from 'https';
import { readFileSync } from 'fs';

// Usar certificados auto-firmados en desarrollo
const httpsAgent = new https.Agent({
  ca: readFileSync('/path/to/ca-cert.pem'),
  cert: readFileSync('/path/to/client-cert.pem'),
  key: readFileSync('/path/to/client-key.pem'),
  rejectUnauthorized: true  // Siempre true en producciÃ³n
});

const response = await fetch('https://api.company.internal/secure', {
  agent: httpsAgent,
  headers: {
    'Authorization': `Bearer ${process.env.API_TOKEN}`,
    'X-Client-Version': 'claude-code/2.1.0'
  }
});

Rotation AutomÃ¡tica de API Keys

#!/bin/bash
# Ejecutar mensualmente con cron

VAULT_ADDR="https://vault.company.com"
OLD_KEY=$(vault kv get -field=ANTHROPIC_API_KEY secret/claude-code/prod)

# 1. Generar nueva key
NEW_KEY=$(curl -s -X POST https://api.anthropic.com/rotate-key \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $OLD_KEY" \
  -H "X-Client-Id: claude-code-prod" | jq -r '.key')

# 2. Actualizar en Vault
vault kv put secret/claude-code/prod ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="$NEW_KEY"

# 3. Notificar administrador
echo "API Key rotated: $(date)" | mail -s "Key Rotation Alert" admin@company.com

# 4. Revocar key antigua (automÃ¡ticamente en 24h)
curl -s -X POST https://api.anthropic.com/revoke-key \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $NEW_KEY" \
  -d "old_key=$OLD_KEY"

1. Sentry: Error Tracking en Tiempo Real

Setup Inicial

npm install @sentry/node @sentry/tracing
# Para Node.js
npm install @sentry/cli --save-dev

InicializaciÃ³n en AplicaciÃ³n

import * as Sentry from "@sentry/node";
import { nodeProfilingIntegration } from "@sentry/profiling-node";

// Inicializar ANTES de crear la app
Sentry.init({
  dsn: process.env.SENTRY_DSN, // https://xxxxx@sentry.io/project
  environment: process.env.NODE_ENV || "development",
  tracesSampleRate: process.env.NODE_ENV === "production" ? 0.1 : 1.0,
  profilesSampleRate: 0.1, // 10% de transacciones incluyen profiling
  integrations: [
    nodeProfilingIntegration(),
    new Sentry.Integrations.Http({ tracing: true }),
    new Sentry.Integrations.OnUncaughtException(),
    new Sentry.Integrations.OnUnhandledRejection()
  ],
  beforeSend(event, hint) {
    // Filtrar errores sensibles
    if (event.request?.cookies?.includes("auth")) {
      delete event.request.cookies;
    }
    if (hint.originalException?.message?.includes("password")) {
      return null; // No enviar
    }
    return event;
  }
});

// Capturar errores no manejados
process.on('uncaughtException', (error) => {
  Sentry.captureException(error);
  process.exit(1);
});

Captura Manual de Errores

import * as Sentry from "@sentry/node";

async function processPayment(orderId: string) {
  const transaction = Sentry.startTransaction({
    op: "payment",
    name: "Process Payment",
    data: { orderId }
  });

  try {
    const result = await chargeCard(orderId);
    transaction.setStatus("ok");
    return result;
  } catch (error) {
    // Capturar con contexto
    Sentry.captureException(error, {
      level: "error",
      tags: {
        orderId,
        paymentProvider: "stripe",
        retryCount: 2
      },
      extra: {
        amount: order.total,
        currency: order.currency
      }
    });

    // Alertar a equipo
    await notifySlack(`âŒ Payment failed: ${orderId}`, "critical");
    throw error;
  } finally {
    transaction.finish();
  }
}

// Breadcrumbs para debugging
Sentry.captureMessage("User started checkout", "info");
Sentry.addBreadcrumb({
  category: "payment",
  message: "Stripe API call initiated",
  level: "debug",
  data: { amount: 9999, currency: "USD" }
});

2. DataDog: MÃ©tricas & Tracing Distribuido

Setup Inicial

npm install dd-trace @datadog/browser-rum

InstrumentaciÃ³n

import tracer from 'dd-trace';

// Inicializar ANTES de importar otros mÃ³dulos
tracer.init({
  service: 'claude-code-api',
  env: process.env.NODE_ENV,
  version: '1.2.3',
  logInjection: true,  // Inyectar trace IDs en logs
  sampleRate: process.env.NODE_ENV === 'prod' ? 0.1 : 1.0
});

import express from 'express';
const app = express();

app.use(tracer.middleware.express());

// Cada request es automÃ¡ticamente traced
app.get('/api/refactor', async (req, res) => {
  const span = tracer.activeSpan();
  span?.setTag('user.id', req.user.id);
  span?.setTag('action', 'refactor');

  const child = tracer.startChildSpan('database_query', {
    resource: 'users.findById',
    type: 'db'
  });

  try {
    const user = await db.users.findById(req.user.id);
    child?.finish();
    res.json(user);
  } catch (error) {
    child?.setTag('error', true);
    child?.finish(error);
    res.status(500).json({ error: error.message });
  }
});

export default app;

3. Honeycomb: Observabilidad Ãgil

    name: 'analyze_code',

      'file.path': req.body.filePath,

      'user.id': req.user.id
    }
  });

  try {
    const result = await analyzeCode(req.body.filePath);
    res.json(result);
  } finally {
    span.end();
  }
});

Memory Leak Detection

import { performance } from 'perf_hooks';

setInterval(() => {
  const mem = process.memoryUsage();
  const heapUsed = Math.round(mem.heapUsed / 1024 / 1024);
  const external = Math.round(mem.external / 1024 / 1024);

  console.log(`Memory: ${heapUsed}MB heap, ${external}MB external`);

  // Alerta si sobrepasa umbral
  if (heapUsed > 500) {
    Sentry.captureMessage('High memory usage detected', 'warning', {
      extra: { heapUsed, external }
    });

    // Forzar garbage collection (cuidado en producciÃ³n)
    if (global.gc) {
      global.gc();
    }
  }
}, 30000); // Cada 30 segundos

5. Structured Logging: Logs que se Buscan

Setup con Winston & JSON Logs

import winston from 'winston';
import * as Sentry from '@sentry/node';

const logger = winston.createLogger({
  defaultMeta: { service: 'claude-api', version: '1.2.3' },
  format: winston.format.combine(
    winston.format.timestamp(),
    winston.format.errors({ stack: true }),
    winston.format.json()
  ),
  transports: [
    new winston.transports.File({
      filename: 'logs/error.log',
      level: 'error'
    }),
    new winston.transports.File({
      filename: 'logs/combined.log'
    }),
    new winston.transports.Console({
      format: winston.format.simple()
    })
  ]
});

// Logs estructurados
logger.info('User refactoring started', {
  userId: 'user-123',
  filePath: 'src/auth.ts',
  framework: 'express',
  estimatedDuration: 120  // segundos
});

logger.error('Refactoring failed', {
  userId: 'user-123',
  filePath: 'src/auth.ts',
  errorCode: 'PARSE_ERROR',
  errorMessage: error.message,
  stack: error.stack
});

// IntegraciÃ³n Sentry + Winston
logger.on('error', (error) => {
  Sentry.captureException(error);
});

Log Aggregation con ELK (Elasticsearch, Logstash, Kibana)

{
  "input": {
    "file": {
      "path": "/var/log/claude-api/*.log",
      "sincedb_path": "/var/lib/logstash/.sincedb"
    }
  },
  "filter": {
    "json": {
      "source": "message"
    },
    "mutate": {
      "add_field": {
        "[@metadata][index_name]": "claude-api-%{+YYYY.MM.dd}"
      }
    }
  },
  "output": {
    "elasticsearch": {
      "hosts": ["elasticsearch:9200"],
      "index": "%{[@metadata][index_name]}"
    }
  }
}

6. Alerting Inteligente

Alertas Multicanal

import * as Sentry from '@sentry/node';
import slack from '@slack/web-api';
import pagerduty from '@pagerduty/pdjs';

class AlertManager {
  private slackClient = new slack.WebClient(process.env.SLACK_BOT_TOKEN);

  async alertError(error: Error, severity: 'warning' | 'critical') {
    const message = {
      text: `${severity === 'critical' ? 'ðŸš¨' : 'âš ï¸'} ${error.message}`,
      blocks: [
        {
          type: 'header',
          text: {
            type: 'plain_text',
            text: severity === 'critical' ? 'CRITICAL ERROR' : 'Warning'
          }
        },
        {
          type: 'section',
          text: {
            type: 'mrkdwn',
            text: `*Error:* \`${error.message}\`\n*Stack:* \`\`\`${error.stack}\`\`\``
          }
        },
        {
          type: 'actions',
          elements: [
            {
              type: 'button',
              text: { type: 'plain_text', text: 'View in Sentry' },
              url: 'https://sentry.io/...'
            }
          ]
        }
      ]
    };

    // Enviar a Slack
    await this.slackClient.chat.postMessage({
      channel: severity === 'critical' ? '#alerts-critical' : '#alerts',
      ...message
    });

    // Si es crÃ­tico, escalar a PagerDuty
    if (severity === 'critical') {
      await pagerduty.events.trigger({
        routing_key: process.env.PAGERDUTY_ROUTING_KEY,
        dedup_key: `error-${Date.now()}`,
        event_action: 'trigger',
        payload: {
          summary: error.message,
          severity: 'critical',
          source: 'claude-api',
          custom_details: {
            stack: error.stack
          }
        }
      });
    }
  }
}

export const alertManager = new AlertManager();

Anomaly Detection

monitors:
  - name: "High Error Rate"
    type: "metric alert"
    query: "avg:trace.web.request.errors{service:claude-api}.as_count() > 10"
    threshold: 10
    timeframe: "5m"
    severity: "critical"

  - name: "Memory Leak Detected"
    type: "metric alert"
    query: "avg:process.runtime.node.memory.heap.used{service:claude-api}"
    alert_threshold: 600000000  # 600MB
    warning_threshold: 500000000
    timeframe: "10m"

  - name: "Response Time Degradation"
    type: "forecast alert"
    query: "avg:trace.web.request.duration{service:claude-api}"
    forecast_type: "linear"
    alert_threshold: 2000  # 2 segundos
    timeframe: "1h"

7. Debugging en ProducciÃ³n: Best Practices

TÃ©cnicas Avanzadas

  • Remote Debugging: Conectar VS Code a proceso remoto con Node Inspector
  • Canary Deployments: Enviar 10% trÃ¡fico a versiÃ³n nueva, monitorear mÃ©tricas
  • Feature Flags: Activar/desactivar features sin redeploy (LaunchDarkly, Unleash)
  • Request Sampling: Loguear 100% de errores, 5% de requests normales
  • Context Propagation: Mantener trace ID a travÃ©s de servicios distribuidos

Feature Flags para Testing en ProducciÃ³n

import LaunchDarkly from '@launchdarkly/node-server-sdk';

const ldClient = new LaunchDarkly.LDClient(process.env.LAUNCHDARKLY_KEY);

async function refactorCode(userId: string, filePath: string) {
  // Verificar si usuario estÃ¡ en experimento
  const useNewRefactorEngine = await ldClient.variation(
    'new-refactor-engine',
    { key: userId },
    false
  );

  if (useNewRefactorEngine) {
    console.log('Using new refactor engine (beta)');
    return await refactorWithNewEngine(filePath);
  } else {
    return await refactorWithStableEngine(filePath);
  }
}

// Metricas: rastrear adoption del feature flag
if (useNewRefactorEngine) {
  statsd.increment('feature.new_refactor_engine.used');
}

Nivel 5 / Troubleshooting

ðŸ”§ Troubleshooting Masterclass

De "Â¿quÃ© saliÃ³ mal?" a "lo sÃ© en 5 minutos"

Cuando Claude Code falla en producciÃ³n, el tiempo es crÃ­tico. Este mÃ³dulo te enseÃ±a a diagnosticar cinco categorÃ­as de fallo comÃºn en menos de 5 minutos usando un Ã¡rbol de decisiÃ³n estructurado.

5 SÃ­ntomas de Alerta Roja

  • Token leak: Contexto crece ilimitadamente (P99 latency, OOM en 2-3 horas)
  • Hook failures: JSON invÃ¡lido, timeouts, handshake errors
  • MCP connection issues: Stdio buffer deadlock, pool exhaustion
  • Agent slowness: P50 = 200ms, P99 = 5s (varianza 25Ã—)
  • Cost explosion: Presupuesto diario se duplica en 24h

Diagnosis en 5 Pasos

  1. Paso 1: Â¿CuÃ¡ndo comenzÃ³? (timeline)
  2. Paso 2: Â¿QuÃ© mÃ©trica cambiÃ³? (logs, CPU)
  3. Paso 3: Â¿QuÃ© sistemas afecta? (scope)
  4. Paso 4: Â¿QuÃ© cambiÃ³ 24h antes? (commits)
  5. Paso 5: Â¿CÃ³mo validamos fix? (antes/despuÃ©s)

3ï¸âƒ£ MCP Connection Issues

SÃ­ntoma #1: Pool Exhaustion

âŒ ANTES: Sin cierre de conexiÃ³n

class MCPClient:
    def send_request(self, payload):
        conn = self.pool.acquire()
        self.connections.append(conn)  # â† NUNCA se libera
        return conn.send(payload)

# Resultado: 1000 requests â†’ 1000 conexiones â†’ OVERFLOW

âœ… DESPUÃ‰S

def send_request(self, payload):
    with self.pool.acquire() as conn:  # â† Auto-close
        return conn.send(payload)

SÃ­ntoma #2: Stdio Deadlock

âŒ ANTES: Buffer lleno, sin lectura

proc.stdin.write(b"LARGE_PAYLOAD")  # â† Buffer lleno
# proc.stdout.read() nunca se ejecuta â†’ DEADLOCK

âœ… DESPUÃ‰S: Non-blocking I/O

def send_request(self, payload, timeout=5):
    self.proc.stdin.write(payload.encode() + b"\n")
    self.proc.stdin.flush()

    start = time.time()
    while time.time() - start < timeout:
        if self.last_response:
            return self.last_response
        time.sleep(0.01)

    raise TimeoutError()

4ï¸âƒ£ Agent Slowness: P50 vs P99

  data[NR] = $NF
}

  n = NR

  print "P50:", p50, "ms"
  print "P99:", p99, "ms"
}'

# Output:
# P50: 180 ms
# P99: 4200 ms (23Ã— mÃ¡s lento)

Causa: Cache Misses

âŒ ANTES: Sin cachÃ©

    result = await claude.messages.create(...)  # â† 1.2s
    return result  # Total: 3.2s

# Latencias: Hit 1 = 3.2s, Hit 2 (warm) = 1.2s
# P50 = 1.2s, P99 = 3.2s

âœ… DESPUÃ‰S: Con cachÃ© 5 minutos

class CachedUserData:
    async def get_user(self, user_id):
        if self.is_cached(user_id):
            return self.cache[user_id]  # â† 1ms
        data = await db.get_user(user_id)  # â† 2s (raro)
        self.cache[user_id] = data
        return data

# Resultado: P50 = 1.2s, P99 = 1.2s (predecible)

Nivel 5 / Cost Operations

ðŸ’° Cost Forecasting & Operations

De "cuesta mucho" a "presupuesto validado"

Domina cost modeling, forecasting con 90% accuracy, budget allocation y optimizaciÃ³n. Este mÃ³dulo te enseÃ±a a reducir costos 50-70% sin sacrificar calidad, presupuestar por equipo y monitorear en producciÃ³n como una empresa. Tiempo estimado: 90-120 minutos. Requisito: L5.1-5.5, L4.5 (Token Optimization).

ðŸ“ SecciÃ³n 1: Cost Modeling 101

La fÃ³rmula base es simple. Hay 5 levers de control. Y nÃºmeros reales para cada modelo.

FÃ³rmula Base (Total Cost) 
Total Cost =
  (Input Tokens Ã— $0.003) +           // Input rate
  (Cache Write Ã— $0.00375) +           // 25% surcharge
  (Cache Read Ã— $0.0003) +             // 90% descuento
  (Output Tokens Ã— $0.012)             // Output 4Ã— mÃ¡s caro
Ejemplo Real: Summarizar 100 pÃ¡ginas
  • Request 1 (with cache setup): 50K input Ã— $0.003 = $0.15 + Cache write $0.1875 + 500 output Ã— $0.012 = $0.006 â†’ Total: $0.3435
  • Request 2-5 (reuse cache): 50K read (cached) Ã— $0.0003 = $0.015 + 500 output Ã— $0.012 = $0.006 â†’ Total: $0.021 cada (90% discount!)
  • Savings: 5 requests sin cachÃ© = $1.725 | Con cachÃ© = $0.405 â†’ 77% ahorro
5 Levers de Control
  • Model Selection: 5-10Ã— (Opusâ†’Sonnet)
  • Caching: 40-50% savings
  • Batch API: 15-25% discount
  • Context Compression: 25-35% reduction
  • Agent Reuse: 20-30% savings
Costo/Min por Modelo
  • Opus 4.7: $0.18/min (cached)
  • Sonnet 4.6: $0.048/min
